# Issues Done Archive

Resolved entries (`- [x] **[DONE]**`) moved here from `Issues-and-TODOs.md` on Tuesday, October 20, 2026 — 15:01 PHT, **unchanged**, under the headings they had there. Nothing was edited or deleted: each line is exactly as it was, with its `Resolved <timestamp>` and its session link. The headings keep their original text and order; a heading appears once for each place it had resolved entries.

New resolved entries are moved here when they are resolved (see "Format for new entries" in `Issues-and-TODOs.md`). The open counts and the Done total are in that file's summary block. When an open entry refers to a resolved one by title, search this file.

## Lead entry under the summary block (it has no section heading of its own)

- [x] **[DONE]** **New Visit full-suite tests fail shortly after midnight** (found Thursday, October 08, 2026 — 00:04 PHT): four `NewVisitEntryPage` tests expect a newly saved visit to sort ahead of the seed's same-day visits, but the mock timestamp at 00:03 sorts before seed records later that day. Focused incident tests pass; this is pre-existing, time-of-day-sensitive test behavior and was not changed in the Incident Stage 2 work. Resolved Sunday, October 11, 2026 — 20:31 PHT: the tests find the saved visit by identity (its `submit` audit entry, `getSavedVisit()`), not by newest-by-time; app sorting unchanged. Evidence: `NewVisitEntryPage.test.tsx` under a fixed clock (Date-only fake timers): original 4 failed / 11 passed at 00:03, fixed 15/15 at 00:03 and 15/15 at 15:30. - [x] **[DONE]** **Additional backup layer beyond local + external drive** — team is still evaluating what this should be (possibly off-site/cloud). Not yet decided. Update Thursday, October 22, 2026 — 15:51 PHT: the panel review (BK-1, BK-2) says one external drive plugged into the same PC is exposed to fire, theft, power surges and ransomware: at least two copies on different media or places (the drive plus a copy held by the Principal or an IT custodian), encrypted, with a short written custody procedure; and back up at sign-out or shutdown as well as 2:00 AM, because school PCs are switched off overnight. Part 6 adds "Back up now" and a stale-backup alert. See the new "Who is told when a backup is stale, and who is the IT contact?". Session: [2026-10-22-panel-review-triage-and-build-prompt](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-22-panel-review-triage-and-build-prompt.md) Update Sunday, October 25, 2026 — 16:58 PHT: Sir Rey said a cloud backup should already exist and Sir Mina asked where the backup goes. Zean ruled out the cloud (ADR-039) and said there is no plan for extra copies yet: the backup is the internal and external drive only. He first agreed to a copy held by the Principal (Sunday, October 25, 2026 — 16:45) and withdrew it at 16:57. The question of any further layer stays open and is not planned. Resolved Thursday, October 29, 2026 — 22:18 PHT: Zean: use the internal and external drives, let the clinician choose, add and edit which drives are backed up, and create the folders automatically in a sensible structure. No extra copy and no cloud (ADR-039 stands). ADR-052. Build: the `FIX` "Backup: choose, add and edit the backup locations". Session: [2026-10-29-open-decisions-answered](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-29-open-decisions-answered.md)


## Login redesign follow-ups — Saturday, October 03, 2026 — 02:11 PHT

- [x] **[DONE]** **Official school photo for the Login backdrop.** Updated Saturday, October 03, 2026 — 02:21 PHT: a stand-in is now in place at `frontend/public/brand/login-backdrop.jpg`, a frame from a campus drone video of the main building, blurred in CSS. Replace it with an official Mendez Christian Academy photo when one is available: same path, no code change. The unused alternative `frontend/public/brand/campus-courtyard.jpg` (~0.9 MB) still ships in the build; delete it if it isn't wanted. Resolved Wednesday, October 14, 2026 — 18:00 PHT: **Decided by the agent, the team may overrule.** Chose to keep the stand-in and its treatment (CSS `blur-sm` + 45% `brand-green-dark` overlay) and to delete the unused `frontend/public/brand/campus-courtyard.jpg` (~0.9 MB; no code references it, only past logs). Options considered: keep everything (ships a dead 0.9 MB file); delete the courtyard file (chosen); rework the blur/overlay (no legibility problem to solve). Numbers: Login axe none at 375/390/768/1280, no overflow. Looked like: the white card stands clearly apart from the blurred green photo at every width; labels and the Sign In button read at a glance. Why: nothing about the stand-in hurts legibility; the extra file is pure weight. Would reverse if: the team wants the courtyard shot as the backdrop (restore it from git history). Swapping in the official photo stays open as a `LATER` item. Session: [2026-10-14-open-fix-and-ui-decide](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-14-open-fix-and-ui-decide.md)
- [x] **[DONE]** **Force Password Change (#2) still uses the old plain gray layout**, so the first-login flow changes look between Login and that screen. Left as-is by the requester's choice (Login-only scope); decide whether it should share the backdrop. Resolved Wednesday, October 14, 2026 — 18:00 PHT: **Decided by the agent, the team may overrule.** Chose to give Force Password Change the Login treatment: the same `LoginBackdrop`, the `BrandLogo` header with a divider, and the overlay card shadow, also on its loading card. Options considered: leave the gray page (the look jumps right after Sign In); add only the logo (still a jump); share the backdrop (chosen, reuses Login's components, no new code). Why: consistency of the first-login flow at no cost. Would reverse if: the team wants a deliberately distinct "setup" look. Evidence: `ForcePasswordChangePage.test.tsx`; screenshots d-fpc-375/1280. ## Resolved since last update

- [x] **[DONE]** **Wednesday, October 07, 2026 — 21:48 PHT — Visit Detail's Complaint edit was an unnormalized free textarea.** It now uses the same complaint `Combobox` (shared `ComplaintField`) and the same normalization as New Visit, and `updateVisit` in the data layer normalizes too, so an edit can't reintroduce a spelling or case variant.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Wednesday, October 07, 2026 — 01:16 PHT — Report an Issue recipient email.** Replaced the footer modal's `mailto:` fallback recipient `team@example.com` with `zkg.balboa@gmail.com` in `frontend/src/features/issue-reports/reportIssueConfig.ts`, with the modal regression assertion updated to match.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Saturday, October 3, 2026 — 14:52 PHT — Dashboard trend table relied on `min-width: max-content` with `table-layout: fixed`.** That pairing worked in Chromium but isn't consistently defined across browsers. The table now keeps `fixedLayout`, which keeps its column widths stable under the zero-activity toggle, and overflows through an explicit `minTableWidth` equal to its column sum.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Saturday, October 3, 2026 — 14:13 PHT — Dashboard trend granularity could contradict the date range.** Daily was selectable on All (re-creating the 50+ column problem) and Yearly on Today. The manual toggle is removed; `trendGranularityFor` derives the bucket size from the range preset and span. An empty All range no longer builds monthly buckets back to 1900.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Saturday, October 3, 2026 — 13:58 PHT — Dashboard Alerts lists overflowed in print.** Print lifts each `ListCard` height cap, but the Alerts grid kept its fixed `26rem` rows, so full follow-up and inventory lists spilled over the Visits trend / Common complaints row. The Dashboard header now uses the shared Card surface; the Incident Archive now sorts newest-first; the desktop QR hub shows an instructional empty state rather than an idle skeleton; and all shared horizontally scrollable DataTables are keyboard-focusable with a visible focus ring. The completed follow-up/PE-default/stage-1 sign-off items remain product decisions, not implementation defects.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Wednesday, September 30, 2026 — 22:55 — Security/accountability gap found and fixed: the app required no Login.** A never-signed-in browser could open every route, including the QR hubs, Emergency, Incident Stage 1, and Reports; `?role=instructor` impersonated any role; Log out did not block the next visit; and `/force-password-change?user=<id>` could set any account's password. Now a session exists only after Login, every route redirects to Login without one (and returns the user afterwards), a first-login account has no session until its password is changed, and Log out ends access. Recorded in `06-Decisions/ADR-002-QR-Staff-Only-Redesign.md` (security clarification). The backend must enforce the same rule in Phase B2.

### Interaction-based UI/UX review — Wednesday, September 30, 2026 — 19:55

- [x] **[DONE]** **Broken — Incident Entry (#16/#25), Staff.** Tapping the mobile Emergency button (or the QR hub's Emergency button before any scan) → Start Stage 1 opened a form already attached to a hardcoded demo student ("Gian Quizon") with no way to change it, so the incident was filed against the wrong student. Stage 1 now shows a required Student Number field when no student was identified.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Broken — Incident Entry Stage 2 (#16b), Staff.** Stage 2 existed only in the same sitting as Stage 1. A "Needs completion" incident in the Incident Log could never be reopened, although #16b and Reference 5 require reopening the same record later. Added `/incidents/:incidentId/complete` and a "Complete Stage 2" row action. Stage 1 also can no longer be submitted twice, which previously created a duplicate incident.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Broken — Student Profile (#7), Staff.** Clicking Archive did nothing; the button had no handler, and the existing `archiveStudent` API was never called. It now archives after a confirmation dialog and shows the Archived badge; the audit entry is written.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Broken — Visit Log (#10) and Incident Log (#15), Staff.** Both lists silently cut results at 120 rows, so 39 of the 159 seed visits were unreachable under "All". The cap was removed.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Broken — Instructor Lookup (#26) and Staff mobile hub (#23), 390px.** The shell-free mobile screen had no sign-out control, so an Instructor could not log out at all. A red, icon-only Log out control matching the desktop header was added.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Inconsistent — Visit Log and Incident Log.** No pagination, against `cliniq-pagination-patterns`. Both now use the shared `Pagination`, and filter changes reset to page 1.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Inconsistent — Incident Log.** Used separate From/To date inputs defaulting to the last 30 days, against `cliniq-date-range-patterns`. It now uses the shared `DateRangePicker` defaulting to All.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Inconsistent — Incident Report (#18).** Vitals were labelled with raw data keys (`temperatureC`, `pulseBpm`, `treatmentNotes`) and the timestamp was ISO. It now shows plain labels with units and the shared date format.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Inconsistent — Incident Report (#18).** "Approve report", an irreversible sign-off, applied on one click. It now asks for confirmation first, like the Excuse Letter's approval gate.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Inconsistent — New Visit Entry (#11).** A successful Save left the form filled, so a second click recorded the visit twice; Reference 2 says success clears the form. It now clears after every successful save, and the confirmation banner stays.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Inconsistent — Add/Edit User (#34).** Submitting empty did nothing and showed nothing. Inline required-field messages were added, along with an `h1` page header; the page had none.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Inconsistent — Edit User (#34) and Edit Inventory Item (#29).** The load-error state had no retry action. "Try again" was added.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Inconsistent — Login (#1) and Force Password Change (#2).** An empty submit showed the browser's native tooltip instead of CLINIQ's inline field errors. They now use inline messages.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Inconsistent — accessibility landmarks.** Nine shell screens (QR desktop, QR print, Reports, Audit Log, Users, Add User, Backup, Parent Notifications, Incident Report) nested a second `<main>` inside the shell's, and the Instructor profile had none (axe `landmark-no-duplicate-main`, `landmark-one-main`). Each screen now has exactly one `main`.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Inconsistent — Follow-Up List (#18c).** The Cancelled status badge had no icon, while every other status badge has one. It now has one.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Inconsistent — QR mobile hub (#23), 390px.** Scan QR Code was desktop-sized (40px) although Reference 4 makes it the dominant, thumb-sized control; it is now 56px on phones. The floating "Shortcuts ?" keyboard-help button covered "Look up student"; it is now hidden below `md`.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Cosmetic — QR quick actions.** "Dispense Medicine" used a grey neutral style unlike its sibling actions, and "Log Emergency" had no horizontal padding. Both now match.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Cosmetic — grade-level dropdowns (Student List, Add/Edit Student).** "Kinder" was sorted after "Grade 12". It now comes first.
- [x] **[DONE]** **Inconsistent — no navigation below the `lg` breakpoint.** On shell screens the sidebar is hidden with no menu replacement. This matters for screens opened from mobile QR quick-actions (New Visit, Incident Entry, Dispense, Profile), where only browser Back leaves the screen. Decide whether mobile shell navigation is in scope. Resolved Wednesday, October 14, 2026 — 18:00 PHT: **Decided by the agent, the team may overrule.** Chose: mobile shell navigation **is in scope**, and the role-aware "Open navigation" menu built on October 1 stays (ADR-022). Two gaps found and fixed: the PE/Sports Instructor (no menu entries; sees the shell only on the privacy page) got an empty "Navigation" dialog, so the button is now hidden for a role with no entries; and phones had no way to reach Privacy Policy or Report an Issue, so the menu now ends with both. Options considered: out of scope / remove the menu (QR quick-action screens left with only browser Back); keep the dialog menu (chosen, already uses the shared Modal with focus trap and Escape); a bottom tab bar or slide-in drawer (new patterns, no gain). Numbers: menu entries and footer links 44px tall; no overflow and axe none at 375/390 (Staff) and 375 (Instructor). Looked like: one tidy list with the footer links under a divider at the bottom, reachable with one scroll on a 390px phone. Why: QR flows land Staff on shell screens on a phone. Would reverse if: the team limits phone use to the QR hub only. Evidence: `AppShell.test.tsx` ("reaches Privacy Policy and Report an Issue from the mobile menu", "shows no menu button to a role with no menu entries"); `cliniq-sidebar-patterns` gained a section. Session: [2026-10-14-open-fix-and-ui-decide](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-14-open-fix-and-ui-decide.md)

## Free-text complaints and student lookup — Wednesday, October 07, 2026 — 20:03 PHT

- [x] **[DONE]** ~~**Visit Detail's Complaint edit is still an unnormalized free textarea.**~~ Resolved Wednesday, October 07, 2026 — 21:48 PHT (see Resolved since last update).
- [x] **[DONE]** **Typing a full Student Number and clicking Save without picking** no longer identifies the student (the old field did). Enter, or a click on the result, picks it; Save without a pick asks for one. Resolved Wednesday, October 14, 2026 — 18:00 PHT: **Decided by the agent, the team may overrule.** Chose to keep the explicit pick (no change). Options considered: keep the pick, so Enter or a click picks and Save without a pick asks for one (current); auto-resolve an exact full Student Number on Save. Numbers: none (no visual change). Looked like: not needed; no visual change. Why: the pick shows the student's name before anything is saved (a deliberate single lookup, `cliniq-display-privacy`), so a one-digit typo that matches another real student is caught; auto-resolving would attach a visit, incident, or dispense to a student nobody saw confirmed, and the save's audit entry would point at the wrong record. Pressing Enter already picks an exact match, so the cost is one key. Would reverse if: real use shows the extra key slows Staff, then auto-resolve only with the name shown in a confirmation. Session: [2026-10-14-open-fix-and-ui-decide](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-14-open-fix-and-ui-decide.md)

- [x] **[DONE]** **No warning for unusual vitals** (Review #9, MEDIUM). In the Incident form, temperature 34 and pulse 35 were accepted with no message; `validateStageOne` in `IncidentEntryPage.tsx` only checks that the values are present. Add a non-blocking warning ("this looks unusual, please check"), never a block, so fast capture is not slowed. The thresholds themselves are a clinical decision (see below); build the warning so the ranges come from one config value. Resolved Wednesday, October 14, 2026 — 18:00 PHT: Incident Stage 1 shows a non-blocking amber note ("Temperature 370 °C looks unusual. Please check the reading. You can still save.") when a reading falls outside `config.vitalsWarningRanges` in `mock-db.json`. Those ranges are **placeholder sanity bounds, not clinical thresholds, nurse to confirm** (temperature 30–43 °C, pulse 30–250 bpm), labelled so in the config, so they catch typing slips such as 370 for 37.0; the reviewer's 34 °C / 35 bpm will not warn until the nurse sets real thresholds (the DECIDE "Unusual-vitals thresholds" stays open). Evidence: `IncidentEntryPage.test.tsx` ("warns about an implausible reading but still saves Stage 1", "shows no warning for an ordinary reading"); integrity check validates the new config key. - [x] **[DONE]** **Should an expired medicine's stock drop to zero by itself?** (second review, page 2). The reviewer asks whether flagging an item as Expired could zero its stock, and recalls the nurse checking and removing expired medicines one by one in her free time. Recommendation: **do not auto-zero.** ADR-018 deliberately keeps the quantity until someone disposes of the item through Adjust Stock with the reason "Expired - disposed", which leaves an audit record of what was thrown away; zeroing silently would erase that record and would not match what is physically still on the shelf. Giving expired items out is already blocked. A workable shortcut to decide with the nurse: a "Dispose" action on an expired row (and optionally "Dispose all expired") that opens Adjust Stock with the reason and the full quantity pre-filled, for her to confirm. Resolved Saturday, October 17, 2026 — 12:09 PHT: ADR-030 requires explicit batch disposal; expiry never auto-zeros stock. Session: [2026-10-17-professor-consultation-part-1-inventory-batches](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-17-professor-consultation-part-1-inventory-batches.md)

Professor [presentation 00:55:27 to 00:58:56], who called this "very critical": restocking an item that has expired stock merges the new quantity into one total and the expired batch disappears, so expired medicine could be given. The mock data and the ERD draft hold one expiration date per item, not per batch. Needed: batches (quantity and expiry each), expired stock shown separately, a clear Disposed record and history (reason, quantity, who), an Adjust Stock flow that does not hide an expired batch, and dispensing from the non-expired batch first. Related: ADR-018 (amend it), the open DECIDE "Should an expired medicine's stock drop to zero by itself?", and the ERD draft (add a batch table and log it as an open decision). Session: [2026-10-16-professor-consultation-feedback](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-16-professor-consultation-feedback.md) Resolved Saturday, October 17, 2026 — 12:09 PHT: ADR-030 adds batch-level mock storage and legacy/persisted-state migration; Restock creates a batch; Inventory shows usable and expired quantities separately, filters expired stock, exposes batch/movement history, and records explicit selected-batch disposal; dispensing uses usable earliest-expiry batches and visit edits return to their source batch. Session: [2026-10-17-professor-consultation-part-1-inventory-batches](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-17-professor-consultation-part-1-inventory-batches.md)

## Meeting with the clinician — Tuesday, October 20, 2026 — 14:58 PHT

- [x] **[DONE]** **Several emergency contacts per student** (logged Tuesday, October 20, 2026 — 14:58 PHT). The Grade 11 case [11:42 to 13:31] showed that the one emergency contact on a student record (name, relationship, phone) can fail: both parents were unreachable for about an hour and the clinic reached the student's graduate sister only by remembering her. The team's own action item from the meeting is to allow several contacts. Decide: how many contacts (two? three? no limit), which relationships (mother, father, guardian, sibling or relative), whether one is marked primary, whether the incident screen and the parent-notification log show all of them, and whether the contact list is required at enrollment or optional. This changes the student form, the student entity (`emergencyContact: EmergencyContact | null` today), the profile and the database design before migrations. Related: "Two contact numbers on a student record". Ask the clinician what she actually needs on hand in an emergency. Session: [2026-10-20-clinician-meeting-findings](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-20-clinician-meeting-findings.md) Resolved Tuesday, October 20, 2026 — 15:16 PHT: a student has a list of emergency contacts, not one; Zean's instruction was "the simplest solution will be to be able to add multiple emergency contacts for students, not just one". Decided: (1) each contact keeps today's fields: name, relationship, phone and the `verified` flag. (2) At least one contact is needed to complete a record (today's rule); more are optional. (3) The first contact in the list is the primary one, and Staff can move another to the top. (4) No fixed maximum is stored; the form starts with one row and has an "Add another contact" button, and an extra row can be removed. (5) Relationship stays typed text with suggestions (Mother, Father, Guardian, Sister or brother, Relative, Other), so the Grade 11 sister can be recorded. (6) The student profile, the parent-notification page and the incident screen list every contact, primary first, each with its own verified state. Not decided here, still open elsewhere: what "Student contact information" means ("Two contact numbers on a student record"), whether a notification attempt records which contact was tried, and the "parents unreachable" wording ("Incident entry: what the clinic actually records in an emergency"). The build is the `FIX` "Allow several emergency contacts per student". Session: [2026-10-20-obvious-decisions-resolved](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-20-obvious-decisions-resolved.md)
- [x] **[DONE]** **Excuse letter: agree on a basis format** (logged Tuesday, October 20, 2026 — 14:58 PHT). The clinician said there is no excuse-letter format or template; letters come from the students and the clinic checks them; nobody has taken the template on [01:43 to 02:22, 05:39 to 06:35], and the team's idea is to set a basis format in the system so letters are consistent. The system already has a letter generator and an A4 print layout (ADR-026) that carries a DRAFT mark until approved, so this is a question about which wording and fields the clinic and the school accept, not about building from nothing. Decide with her and the school: get a sample of a real letter a student brings, who signs it and in what role, what it must say and must not say (it is not a medical certificate), and who approves the format. Related: the DRAFT-mark question. Session: [2026-10-20-clinician-meeting-findings](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-20-clinician-meeting-findings.md) Resolved Thursday, October 29, 2026 — 12:33 PHT: Zean: "common excused letter". Keep the one basic letter format already built (student, dates, reason, clinic signature); the clinic and school approve the wording. - [x] **[DONE]** **Monthly Report and complaint list: follow the clinic's own report** (logged Tuesday, October 20, 2026 — 14:58 PHT). The clinic's report (transcribed in "The clinic's own papers") is one table: complaint rows, four week columns with the clinic's dates (Sept. 1-4, 7-11, 14-18, 21-30, so the last week runs longer), and a total, under the school logo and "MCA CLINIC, MONTHLY CASES REPORT". The system's Monthly Report printout (interim contents, ADR-026) has totals with the previous month, complaints by count and share, dispositions, follow-ups, medicines given, the incident list and signature lines, but **no weekly breakdown**. Decide: (1) add the clinic's weekly complaint table to the printout, and how weeks are cut (Monday to Friday blocks as the clinic does, or calendar weeks), and whether the extra sections stay; (2) the logo and heading wording ("MCA CLINIC" or the system's own); (3) the complaint names. The clinic's list reads Stomach Ache, Head Ache, Fever, Cough & Cold, Wound / Bruise, Body Pain, Vomitting [sic], Dysmenorrhea, Toothache, Dizziness, Diarrhea (the photo is cut off, so there may be more); the system's list reads Stomachache, Headache, Dizziness, Fever, Cough and colds, Menstrual cramps, Minor wound, Toothache, Allergic reaction, Sprain. The system has no Body Pain, Vomiting or Diarrhea type, and the clinic writes Dysmenorrhea where the system writes Menstrual cramps. Adopting the clinic's names as the default list would also reduce the split counts in "Free-text complaints split the counts that group by complaint". Ask the clinician: the rows below the cut-off, what is on Sheet2, and whether she tallies weekly or at month end. - [x] **[DONE]** **Inventory: a monthly sheet like the clinic's notebook** (logged Tuesday, October 20, 2026 — 14:58 PHT). The clinic's page has, per item and month: unit, New Purchase, Total Available and Ending Inventory (transcribed in "The clinic's own papers"). Related: "Inventory: expiry must be tracked per batch". ## Purpose-built A4 printouts (ADR-026) — Thursday, October 15, 2026 — 23:04 PHT

- [x] **[DONE]** **Should printing or saving a PDF be audit-logged?** The Audit Trail requirement lists logins, scans, submissions, approvals, and create/update/delete/archive; printing is not listed, and nothing logs it today. A printout of student health information leaving the clinic may matter for RA 10173. Ask the team; if yes, log it in the `beforeprint` handler with the document title. Update Saturday, October 17, 2026 — 11:02 PHT: the list of events in the requirement includes "scans"; with the QR feature is dropped (ADR-027) there are no scans, so only logins, submissions, approvals and create, update, delete and archive remain. The question about logging prints is unchanged. Session: [2026-10-17-older-entries-conflict-audit](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-17-older-entries-conflict-audit.md) Resolved Tuesday, October 20, 2026 — 15:16 PHT: yes. Printing or saving a PDF of a page that shows student or clinic health information writes one Audit Trail entry with the signed-in user, the real time and the document title (for example "Incident report", "Student profile", "Monthly report"). Reason: a printout takes health data out of the system (RA 10173), and the cost is one entry. It is written from the `beforeprint` handler, so the Print / Save as PDF button and Ctrl+P are both covered. The QR sticker page is not included (the QR feature is dropped, ADR-027). Build: the `FIX` "Audit-log every print and Save as PDF". Session: [2026-10-20-obvious-decisions-resolved](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-20-obvious-decisions-resolved.md)

- [x] **[DONE]** **Rule for Visit versus Incident** (Review #4 HIGH and R1). Fainting, Asthma attack, Fall injury and Severe allergic reaction can be logged as incidents and as visit complaints, so staff may log one event twice or in the wrong module. Proposal to confirm with the nurse: Incident = an emergency needing fast two-stage capture; Visit = routine care. Show that as a one-line hint on both forms. Merging the two forms into one "Case" form with an Emergency switch is a bigger change and probably not worth it. Needs the nurse's rule and probably an ADR; update `Module-Overview.md` and the canonical Modules & Features doc when decided. Also raised by a second reviewer on Tuesday, October 13, 2026 — 23:08. Update Tuesday, October 20, 2026 — 14:58 PHT: the clinician meeting (organized information, `HEALWARE_MS_CLINICIAN`; times in brackets are the recording's, and the recording is about 65% recoverable) did not give a written rule; she said an emergency is decided case by case by her judgment [06:54 to 09:37]. Her examples: a fracture is rushed to emergency; an asthma attack that has not improved in 30 minutes goes to emergency even without the parents' go-ahead; a known heart condition with fainting means calling the parents first, because they decide where to go; a head injury (the Grade 11 case) was kept in the clinic under observation while the parents were being reached. This fits the proposal above (Incident = emergency, Visit = routine) but adds that one case can start as a visit and become an incident, as with asthma after 30 minutes. Still to confirm with her: the proposal itself, and how an asthma visit that escalates should be logged (one record or two). Not resolved. Session: [2026-10-20-clinician-meeting-findings](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-20-clinician-meeting-findings.md) Update Tuesday, October 20, 2026 — 19:38 PHT: the clinic keeps two separate books, a "Clinic Logbook, Daily Visits" and an "Emergency Logbook, when an incident happens" (reported by Zean), which fits Incident = emergency and Visit = routine. How an asthma visit that escalates is logged is still unknown. Not resolved. Session: [2026-10-20-clinic-logbook-fields](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-20-clinic-logbook-fields.md) Update Thursday, October 22, 2026 — 15:51 PHT: the panel review (VS-7) suggests one operational definition and an "Escalate to incident" button on a visit that carries its data over. Zean answered "yes possible" and chose to keep two forms (ADR-036); the button is built in part 2 of `Prompt-Build-Panel-Review-Fixes-Oct22.md`. What is still open is the nurse's own rule for what counts as an emergency (for example ambulance, hospital referral, or a defined list of complaints); it becomes the one-line hint on both forms and the written definition in the defense documents. Session: [2026-10-22-panel-review-triage-and-build-prompt](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-22-panel-review-triage-and-build-prompt.md) Update Thursday, October 22, 2026 — 17:52 PHT: part 2 built "Escalate to incident" and the non-blocking emergency hint (ADR-036). No one-line rule is shown on either form until the clinician gives her wording; a comment in `NewVisitEntryPage.tsx` marks where it goes. Session: [2026-10-22-panel-review-part-2-visit-logic](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-22-panel-review-part-2-visit-logic.md) Update Sunday, October 25, 2026 — 16:58 PHT: Sir Mina also found the two histories redundant and suggested merging them (Sunday, October 25, 2026). He accepted the team's answer that the clinic keeps them separate. Three reviewers have now asked the same thing, which suggests the screens do not explain the difference. ADR-036 stands (two forms and Escalate to incident); if the one-line hint on both forms is not enough, make the "Escalated from visit" link more visible rather than merging. Resolved Thursday, October 29, 2026 — 13:46 PHT: Zean: "common and worse". A Visit is a common case; an Incident is a worse case that needs fast two-stage capture. Keep two forms with the Escalate button (ADR-036) and put a one-line hint on both. ADR-051. Build: the `FIX` "Visit and Incident forms: a one-line hint on which to use". ## The clinic's two logbooks — Tuesday, October 20, 2026 — 19:38 PHT

- [x] **[DONE]** **Daily logbook: what "Parent/Guardian" and "Remarks" mean** (logged Tuesday, October 20, 2026 — 19:38 PHT). The reported daily logbook has these two columns without saying what is written in them. Ask the clinician. Parent/Guardian: the name of the parent or guardian, whether they were informed (and how and when), or who picked the student up? Remarks: the same as our treatment notes, the outcome (for example "sent home"), or something else? The answer decides whether the Visit form gets a parent/guardian field (and whether it becomes the notification log for visits) and whether Remarks needs its own field or is the existing free text. Not built until answered. Session: [2026-10-20-clinic-logbook-fields](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-20-clinic-logbook-fields.md) Resolved Tuesday, October 20, 2026 — 19:48 PHT: Zean's answer, "remarks is just like notes so it can be anything, for parent/guardian I think it's the name". (1) **Remarks** is free-text notes, anything the nurse wants to write; the Visit gets an optional free-text "Remarks" field, kept apart from "Treatment" because the clinic's logbook has both columns. (2) **Parent/Guardian** is the name of the parent or guardian; the Visit gets an optional free-text "Parent/Guardian" name. This is Zean's reading ("I think"), not the clinician's confirmation, and the logbook as reported lists only the column name; if she says it holds something else, reopen it. Not decided here: whether a visit that ends as sent home or referred needs the attempts-and-outcomes notification log that incidents have ("Parent / guardian notification log for visits" stays open). Build: added to the `FIX` "Add Body temperature, Time out and Assisted by to the Visit form" (see its Update). Details discussed but interpreted from a garbled passage: a "Report" button, a choice by section, a printout with health information and recent incidents, the LRN as the identifier. Decide: per section or per chosen student, what goes on it (minimum needed for physical activity, such as allergies, conditions that limit exercise, medicines, emergency contact, versus the full history), how long it stays valid, and who gets a copy. The clinician (Staff only) builds a saved, reusable and editable list of students for a PE/Sports activity; the printout shows each student's full name and grade, their visits and incidents in a look-back window (default 90 days; 30, 60, 90 or the school year), the clinician's optional note per student and, only if she ticks it, the student's recorded conditions. It stores the list and notes, not a copy of medical content, and is computed when printed with an "as of" time. Full names are an exception to the LRN-instead-of-name rule for lists, decided by Zean. Open: whether the nurse and the Principal agree to what is released (the two entries "Ask the nurse what she would release to a PE instructor" and "Ask the Principal about PE instructors" stay open), and the new `DECIDE` "Sports Report: all visits and incidents, or injuries only?". ## The Sports Report for the PE instructor — Wednesday, October 21, 2026 — 00:28 PHT

- [x] **[DONE]** **Build the Sports Report (saved student lists printed for the PE instructor)** (logged Wednesday, October 21, 2026 — 00:28 PHT). Decided in ADR-034 and the resolved entry "What the PE Instructor's printout holds, and how the clinician builds it". The clinician builds a saved, reusable and editable Sports Report: a title, the activity, who requested it, the students (full name and grade; added one by one or a whole grade level at once), an optional note per student, a look-back window (30, 60, 90 days or the school year; default 90) and a tick-box "include recorded conditions" (off by default). The A4 printout (ADR-026) shows, per student, the visits and incidents in the window (date, what happened, outcome), the note and, if ticked, the conditions, with an "as of" time, a confidentiality line and signature lines for the clinician and the receiving instructor. Staff only; the Admin cannot open it. Saved lists store only the list and notes, never a copy of medical content. Creating, editing, archiving and printing are audited. It is part 6 of `Prompt-Build-Removals-QR-LRN-Mobile-Instructor-UserMgmt.md`, to be run after part 5 (LRN). Session: [2026-10-21-sports-report-and-account-answers](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-21-sports-report-and-account-answers.md) Resolved Wednesday, October 21, 2026 — 07:26 PHT: built as ADR-034 is written. Reports > Sports Reports (Staff only; the Admin/Principal is sent to the Dashboard and refused by the data layer): a saved-reports list (open, edit, duplicate, archive and restore; archived hidden by default), the builder (title, activity, requested by, look-back window 30/60/90 days or the school year with 90 the default, "Include recorded conditions and allergies" off by default, students by name or LRN search or a whole grade with a confirm, no repeats, a labelled note per student), and a report view whose preview is the printout's own table. The A4 printout is computed when printed (the Print button reads the visits and incidents again), alphabetical, newest entry first, "No visits or incidents recorded in this period" for a student with none, its own confidentiality footer and signature lines for the clinician and the instructor; it never prints notes, vitals, medicines, contacts, guardians, letters or the LRN. Saved reports hold the list and notes only. Create, edit, duplicate, archive and every print are audited (a print names the report and the number of students). Session: [2026-10-21-sports-report](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-21-sports-report.md)

## Open design questions — school year handling & grade promotion

- [x] **[DONE]** **Grade-level promotion at year rollover.** Does the system bulk-promote every active student one grade level at the start of a new school year? Who triggers it — Staff clicks a button, or does it happen automatically on a date? What about a student repeating a grade — how does Staff exclude them from the bulk promotion? Update Saturday, October 17, 2026 — 08:08 PHT: at the earlier meeting the Principal said students move up by academic performance and go automatically onto the new master list [I 10:08]. The school makes a new master list every school year, so promotion arrives with the yearly list, not through a clinic-side button (reading, not stated). How a student repeating a grade appears was not discussed. Session: [2026-10-17-principal-clinic-meeting-findings](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-17-principal-clinic-meeting-findings.md) Resolved Thursday, October 29, 2026 — 12:33 PHT: Zean: "do suggested". No bulk promotion; grade and section come from the yearly list import (ADR-047). Session: [2026-10-29-decisions-sheet-answers-applied](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-29-decisions-sheet-answers-applied.md)
- [x] **[DONE]** **Section reassignment.** Sections often get reshuffled each year independent of grade level. Same question: bulk operation, manual per-student, or does this just come from a Registrar re-import? Update Saturday, October 17, 2026 — 08:08 PHT: sections are not fixed from year to year: in elementary, first enrolled and first paid decides the morning session or first section, parents may request another section, and reservations start around February [I 10:08]. Sections therefore change with the yearly list. Session: [2026-10-17-principal-clinic-meeting-findings](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-17-principal-clinic-meeting-findings.md) Resolved Thursday, October 29, 2026 — 12:33 PHT: Zean: sections may change yearly. Sections come from the yearly list import (ADR-047). Session: [2026-10-29-decisions-sheet-answers-applied](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-29-decisions-sheet-answers-applied.md)
- [x] **[DONE]** **A repeatable Registrar sync, not just a one-time import.** At the start of each year: some students are new (need a Student Number generated), some are continuing (need grade/section updated), some have left (need archiving). Is that one combined yearly process, or three separate manual actions? Update Saturday, October 17, 2026 — 08:08 PHT: the school makes a new master list every year, sometimes copied and pasted when the students are the same, but its arrangement changes because of transferees [I 03:50, 10:08]; the registrar tells the clinic about mid-year additions [I 07:55]. The school's move-up has no "dropped" flag, so comparing the new list with the old one is the realistic way to find who left (reading). The Principal will prepare the data after a data privacy agreement [I 00:00]. Session: [2026-10-17-principal-clinic-meeting-findings](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-17-principal-clinic-meeting-findings.md) Resolved Thursday, October 29, 2026 — 12:33 PHT: Zean: the suggested answer. One yearly import: add new students, update continuing ones, and flag students missing from the new list (ADR-047). Open: whether those are archived automatically or by the clinician (see the NOTE "Answers left open because they were unclear or blank"). Session: [2026-10-29-decisions-sheet-answers-applied](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-29-decisions-sheet-answers-applied.md)
- [x] **[DONE]** **Historical grade-level accuracy — a real data-integrity consequence, not just a preference.** If a Visit or Incident record reads grade level live off the Student record rather than snapshotting it at the time of the visit, an old visit from when a student was in Grade 7 will show "Grade 8" once they're promoted — because nothing captures what grade they were in *at the time*. Matters if historical reporting by grade level matters ("how many Grade 7 students visited last year"); if it doesn't, current behavior is fine as-is. This is a real choice, not a default to assume either way. Resolved Thursday, October 29, 2026 — 12:33 PHT: Zean: "yes, needed to have an accurate grade and section of that student at the time of the visit". Visits and incidents keep the grade and section at the time (ADR-047). Build: the `FIX` "Visits and incidents keep the grade and section at the time". Session: [2026-10-29-decisions-sheet-answers-applied](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-29-decisions-sheet-answers-applied.md)
- [x] **[DONE]** **Re-enrollment mechanics.** The 5-year retention window was partly justified by "a transferred student might come back" — but nothing decides what happens then. Does a returning student get their *original* archived Student Number un-archived and reused, or a brand-new `YYYY-NNNNN` (since the format is literally "enrollment year")? If the latter, is the old archived record ever linked to the new one? Update Saturday, October 17, 2026 — 08:08 PHT: returning students were not discussed at the earlier meeting [school question 8]. The Student Number part is moot (ADR-027, ADR-028); ERD decision D-11 (restore the archived record) still applies. For retention see "Retention: which group do health records belong to?" in the next section. ## Meeting with the Principal and the clinic, around October 14: what we learned and what is still open — Saturday, October 17, 2026 — 08:08 PHT

- [x] **[DONE]** **Will the team supply the computer, as the Principal suggested?** (logged Saturday, October 17, 2026 — 08:08 PHT). The Principal suggested the team provide a system unit connected to the internet; a monitor is not a problem; the ID system has its own system unit and three servers [P 34:45]. The school has not promised a computer. Decide: whether the team supplies one, who pays, who installs and updates it, where it sits (the clinic is in the registrar's room), a backup drive and a power backup, and what support the team promises after handover (the Principal wants someone reachable when it breaks). Without an answer the Deployment shape entry stays open. Session: [2026-10-17-principal-clinic-meeting-findings](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-17-principal-clinic-meeting-findings.md) Resolved Thursday, October 29, 2026 — 12:33 PHT: Zean: "resolved, they will provide the computer, we will be the one deploying it". Session: [2026-10-29-decisions-sheet-answers-applied](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-29-decisions-sheet-answers-applied.md)
- [x] **[DONE]** **Can the Principal's network reach the clinic computer, and is the clinic connected to the internet?** (logged Saturday, October 17, 2026 — 08:08 PHT). The Principal asked for a unit "connected to the internet" and for a plan for internet loss [P 33:35, 34:45], which suggests the school expects the clinic to have internet. The team's October 16 meeting assumed a local-only system (ADR-029). **Correction from Zean, Saturday, October 17, 2026 — 10:52 PHT:** the Principal's office has Wi-Fi, but it is a different network from the clinic's, so he cannot reach a system that runs only on the clinic's local network. So there are two questions for the teacher who manages the school's computers: can the clinic computer be reached from the Principal's Wi-Fi (the two networks connected, or the clinic computer on both), and would the school allow it, since that widens who can reach a system that holds children's health data (see "Who writes and signs the data privacy agreement?")? Clinic question 34 (a steady connection?) was never asked. Find out whether the clinic room has internet and how reliable it is, then decide whether CLINIQ uses it (an off-site backup copy, an email password reset, report access from the Principal's office) or stays offline. If the networks can be connected, the Principal could read the Dashboard from his own office and the sign-in at the clinic computer becomes optional; if the clinic has reliable internet, the email reset and an off-site backup become options; if neither, ADR-029 stands. Record the answer in ADR-029. The Principal will ask the teams for a data privacy agreement and wants it before he prepares the student data [I 00:00]. The school already follows data privacy practice: parents fill in a data privacy form with the enrollment documents, the registrar acts as the privacy officer, and it is all manual [I 19:00]. Nobody on the team owns this yet. Decide who writes it, who signs for the team and for the school, and by when. Until it is signed the master list, and with it the import and any realistic test data, is blocked. Related: `Privacy-Policy.md`, clinic question 28 (parents' written consent), ERD decision D-12 (no column encryption; BitLocker on the workstation). ## Open product decisions from the mock-data layer — Monday, September 28, 2026 — 09:19

- [x] **[DONE]** **Config values now in `mock-db.json`, carried over from the Dashboard mock — not requirements, they need a team or nurse decision:** `frequentVisitorMinVisits` (3), `frequentVisitorWindowDays` (30; the Dashboard uses its selected range instead), `upcomingFollowUpDays` (7), `expiryWarningDays` (30), `clusterMinCount` (8), `clusterRatio` (2), `topComplaints` (5). Also open: whether stock *at* the threshold counts as low (currently strictly below). Update Saturday, October 17, 2026 — 08:08 PHT: the Principal's picture: about 20 students visit the clinic per day (more on event days such as foundation day), most students come about once a week, and a "persistent" visitor comes roughly every other day [P 12:33, 24:27]. The default of 3 visits in 30 days (`frequentVisitorMinVisits`) would therefore flag a large share of ordinary visitors. The nurse should give the number (see "Ask the nurse: the frequent-visitor number and the vitals ranges" in the next section). The school also counts 37 °C as a fever for some students [P 22:50]. Session: [2026-10-17-principal-clinic-meeting-findings](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-17-principal-clinic-meeting-findings.md) Update Sunday, October 25, 2026 — 16:58 PHT: the Frequent-visitor panel on the Overview and in the Clinic Overview Report is hidden by a config switch until the nurse sets the number (FIX "Hide the Frequent-visitor panel until the nurse sets the number"); the other values here are unchanged. Resolved Thursday, October 29, 2026 — 13:46 PHT: Zean, in chat: "do what's best and what you think is appropriate". Claude set these starting values (all in `config`, all for the nurse to confirm): a frequent visitor is 3 or more visits in 14 days (`frequentVisitorMinVisits` 3, `frequentVisitorWindowDays` 14) and the Frequent-visitor panel is switched back on; the follow-up reminder window stays 7 days; the expiry warning stays 30 days; the top complaints stay 5; stock exactly at the threshold counts as low. The Trends chart cluster marker keeps its old numbers; the Dashboard cluster alert uses 3 students in 3 days (ADR-050). ADR-051. Build: the `FIX` "Set the clinic's starting numbers in `config`". Session: [2026-10-29-decisions-sheet-follow-up-answers](Agent-Sessions/2026-10-29-decisions-sheet-follow-up-answers.md)
